Write down the component decomposition explicitly; examiners often award marks for stating that the quantum parameter q = 1 and the quantum group collapses to the universal enveloping algebra.

Positive Characteristic Parabolic Geometric Langlands and Levi Subgroups

In positive characteristic p, the geometric Langlands correspondence for parabolic bundles involves the moduli stack BunG,P of G‑bundles equipped with a reduction to a parabolic subgroup P ⊂ G. The Levi factor L = P/U, where U is the unipotent radical, controls the Hecke functors: they are constructed using the affine flag variety Fl_L of the Levi factor. The geometric Satake equivalence for Fl_L identifies the category of L‑equivariant perverse sheaves on Fl_L with the representation category of the Langlands dual Levi Ĥ. This equivalence is compatible with induction and restriction functors associated to the inclusion Ĥ → Ĝ, reflecting the functoriality of the Langlands correspondence under Levi subgroups. The Frobenius morphism Frob: X → X pulls back a parabolic bundle to a new parabolic bundle whose weights are multiplied by p. This operation is central to the correspondence because it relates the Hecke eigensheaf condition to the action of the Frobenius on the moduli stack. Gaitsgory and Raskin proved that the categorical geometric Langlands conjecture for parabolic bundles in positive characteristic follows from the existence of a p‑adic interpolation of the Hecke eigensheaf condition. The interpolation uses the theory of (φ,Γ)‑modules on the Fargues–Fontaine curve to relate the Frobenius action on BunG,P to the monodromy of the corresponding local system on the dual side. The result yields a precise description of the wild ramification data in terms of the nilpotent orbits of the Levi factor, extending the classical Deligne–Langlands classification to the geometric setting. In particular, the categorical trace of the parabolic Hecke functors computes the character of the corresponding representation of Ĥ, and the Verlinde formula for parabolic conformal blocks follows from the categorical trace formula.

Non‑Reductive Framing and the Geometric Satake Equivalence

Framing a G‑bundle means choosing a trivialization of the bundle at a fixed point x ∈ X, or more generally a reduction of the structure group to a subgroup H ⊂ G at x. When H is non‑reductive, the moduli stack BunG,H of framed bundles is no longer a smooth Artin stack but a derived stack with non‑trivial derived structure. The geometric Satake equivalence for the affine Grassmannian Gr_G identifies the spherical Hecke category DG(O)(Gr_G) with Rep(Ĝ). For a non‑reductive framing subgroup H, the Hecke category is replaced by the category of H(O)‑equivariant D‑modules on Gr_G, which is equivalent to the category of modules over the quantum Hamiltonian reduction of the affine W‑algebra associated to H. Braverman, Finkelberg, and Nakajima constructed a categorical action of the affine braid group on the derived category of coherent sheaves on the nilpotent cone of Ĝ, which categorifies the action of the affine Hecke algebra on the representation ring of Ĝ. This action lifts to an action of categorical Hecke operators on D‑modules on BunG,H, and the Hecke eigensheaf condition becomes the statement that the object is an eigenobject for this action with eigenvalue given by an oper for Ĝ that is compatible with the framing. The quantum parameter q appears as the deformation parameter of the quantum Hamiltonian reduction; at critical level q = 1 the reduction becomes classical and the correspondence reduces to the classical geometric Langlands correspondence for framed bundles. The categorical trace of the framed Hecke functors computes the character of the corresponding representation of Ĝ, and the resulting Verlinde‑type formula involves the equivariant K‑theory of the framed moduli space. This framework also accommodates the Kapustin–Witten interpretation: the framed moduli space corresponds to a boundary condition in the topological B‑model on the Hitchin moduli space, and the category of branes is equivalent to the category of D‑modules on BunG,H.

  • Confusing the mixed characteristic Prym p‑divisible group with the classical Prym variety; the former is a derived object whose truncation is a p‑divisible group.
  • Assuming the critical level Hecke eigensheaf condition for non‑simply connected groups is the same as for simply connected groups; the component twist by π₁(G) is essential.
  • Neglecting the multiplication of parabolic weights by p under Frobenius pullback in positive characteristic; this is the key to the p‑adic interpolation.
  • Treating the non‑reductive framing category as an ordinary Hecke category; it is a quantum Hamiltonian reduction of the affine W‑algebra.
  • Identifying the quantum parameter q at critical level with a generic value; critical level forces q = 1 and the quantum group collapses to the universal enveloping algebra.
  • Overlooking the role of the Feigin–Frenkel center in controlling the central character of Hecke eigensheaves at critical level for non‑simply connected groups.
  • Believing the categorical geometric Satake equivalence for the affine flag variety of a Levi factor is the same as the spherical equivalence; the former involves the full affine braid group action.

These advanced topics illustrate how arithmetic, categorical, and physical perspectives intertwine in the modern geometric Langlands program.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Level Structure and the Categorical Hecke Operators

A level structure on a principal G‑bundle over a smooth projective curve X is a trivialization of the bundle on a formal neighbourhood of a divisor D ⊂ X, or equivalently a reduction of the structure group to a parahoric subgroup of the loop group G((t)) at each point of D. The moduli stack BunG, D of G‑bundles with such a level structure carries a natural action of the categorical Hecke operators associated to the affine Grassmannian GrG with parabolic level. These operators are defined as integral transforms with kernels given by the derived category of D‑modules on the Hecke correspondence HeckeD → BunG, D × BunG, D × X. The categorical Hecke category D(HeckeD) is monoidal under convolution and acts on D(BunG, D). The geometric Langlands conjecture with level structure predicts an equivalence between this Hecke‑module category and the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the moduli stack LocSysĜ, D of Ĝ‑local systems with compatible ramification data at D. The ramification is encoded by a choice of a Levi subgroup L ⊂ Ĝ and a nilpotent orbit in the Lie algebra of L, mirroring the parahoric data on the automorphic side. The proof strategy, developed by Gaitsgory, Raskin, and others, proceeds by constructing a categorical Whittaker model for the Hecke action, showing that the Whittaker category is equivalent to the category of D‑modules on the affine flag variety of Ĝ with prescribed monodromy. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors then computes the character of the corresponding representation of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at the appropriate level, yielding a categorified Verlinde formula. A key technical point is the compatibility of the Hecke action with the factorization structure on the Ran space of X, which forces the Hecke eigensheaf condition to be local in the factorization sense. This locality is what allows the use of factorization homology to reduce the global correspondence to a collection of local statements at each point of D, each of which is a categorical version of the classical Satake isomorphism for the corresponding parahoric subgroup.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Parabolic Structure and the Whittaker Category

Parabolic bundles are G‑bundles equipped with a reduction to a parabolic subgroup P ⊂ G at a finite set of marked points, together with a choice of weights in the dominant cocharacter lattice of the Levi factor L = P/U. The moduli stack BunG, P of such objects is a smooth Artin stack whose derived category of D‑modules carries an action of the parabolic Hecke operators. These operators are defined by modifying the bundle and the parabolic reduction simultaneously at a point away from the marked divisor, and by a more subtle modification at the marked points that respects the weight data. The Whittaker category Whitt(BunG, P) is the full subcategory of D(BunG, P) consisting of objects that are equivariant with respect to the action of the unipotent radical U of P, twisted by a non‑degenerate character ψ : U → Ga. The geometric Casselman–Shalika formula, proved in this setting by Arinkin and Gaitsgory, identifies the Whittaker category with the category of D‑modules on the moduli stack of opers for the Langlands dual group Ĝ with prescribed singularities at the marked points. An oper is a Ĝ‑local system together with a filtration by subbundles satisfying a transversality condition; the space of opers is a finite‑dimensional algebraic variety that serves as the classical limit of the quantum Hitchin system. The Whittaker equivalence is functorial with respect to the Hecke action: the Hecke functors on the automorphic side correspond under the equivalence to the natural action of the spherical Hecke category of Ĝ on D‑modules on the oper moduli space. This compatibility is the categorical incarnation of the statement that Hecke eigensheaves correspond to opers. Moreover, the categorical trace of the parabolic Hecke functors on the Whittaker category computes the character of the corresponding representation of the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) at the critical level q = 1, reproducing the Feigin–Frenkel center. The construction also extends to the case of wild ramification, where the parabolic structure is replaced by an irregular singularity; the Whittaker category is then replaced by a category of D‑modules with irregular singular support, and the oper moduli space is replaced by the space of irregular opers studied by Frenkel and Gross.

Worked Example: Categorical Hecke Action on Parabolic Whittaker Category
Let G = GL2, X = P1, and consider a single marked point x with full parabolic structure (Borel reduction) and generic weight λ. Describe the effect of the spherical Hecke operator Hx at a point y ≠ x on a Whittaker object W ∈ Whitt(BunGL2, B) corresponding to an oper with regular singularity at x.
The spherical Hecke operator Hy is given by the convolution kernel Δy ∈ D(GrGL2) supported on the Schubert cell Gr≤1. Under the Whittaker equivalence, W corresponds to a D‑module M on the oper space OpGL2(x) which is a line bundle twisted by the weight λ. The Hecke action on opers is the geometric Satake action: Hy acts by tensoring M with the standard representation V of GL2 viewed as a D‑module on the point y. Since the oper space for GL2 with one regular singularity is isomorphic to the affine line with coordinate the accessory parameter, the tensor product simply shifts the accessory parameter by the weight of V. Concretely, if the oper connection is ∇ = d + (0 1; u(z) 0) dz with u(z) having a simple pole at x with residue λ, then Hy·W corresponds to the oper with u(z) replaced by u(z) + δ(z−y)·(1,0) where δ is the delta‑function at y. In the categorical language, the functor Hy sends the Whittaker D‑module W to the convolution W ⋆ Δy, which under the equivalence becomes M ⊗ V. The resulting object is again a Whittaker object because the Whittaker condition is preserved by the Hecke action (the unipotent equivariance is compatible with convolution). Thus the Hecke eigenvalue is the oper itself, confirming the eigensheaf property.
Exam‑Day Strategy
When a question asks for the relationship between Hecke operators and opers, first identify the group, the curve, and the ramification type. Then recall that the Whittaker category provides the bridge: Hecke functors act on the automorphic side, and under the Whittaker equivalence they become the geometric Satake action on the oper moduli space. Write down the categorical trace formula Tr(Hy) = χV(oper) to show you understand the character computation. Mention the role of the Feigin–Frenkel center at critical level to earn full marks.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Fixed Determinant and the Prym Variety in Mixed Characteristic

Fix a reductive group G over a finite field 𝔽q and a smooth projective curve X/𝔽q. The moduli stack BunGdet=Λ of G‑bundles with a fixed determinant line bundle Λ is a connected component of BunG. Its ℓ‑adic cohomology carries an action of the geometric Frobenius, and the function–sheaf dictionary translates this into an action of the Hecke operators on the space of automorphic forms. In mixed characteristic, one considers the p‑adic analogue: let X be a curve over a p‑adic field K with ring of integers OK. The moduli stack of G‑bundles with fixed determinant over the special fibre Xk admits a p‑divisible group structure on its Picard variety, and the Prym variety associated to a double cover X̃ → X appears as the kernel of the norm map on Jacobians. The geometric Langlands correspondence in this setting, developed by Fargues–Scholze and extended by Zhu, relates the category of ℓ‑adic sheaves on BunGdet=Λ to the category of coherent sheaves on the stack of Ĝ‑local systems on the Fargues–Fontaine curve XFF. The Prym variety enters because the determinant condition forces the corresponding local system to have trivial determinant, i.e. to land in the derived subgroup Ĝder. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors on the fixed‑determinant category computes the character of the representation of the Langlands dual group on the cohomology of the Prym variety, yielding a p‑adic Verlinde formula. A crucial technical tool is the theory of (φ,Γ)‑modules on XFF, which provides a p‑adic interpolation of the Frobenius action on the moduli of bundles. The interpolation shows that the Hecke eigensheaf condition at level n corresponds to a condition on the monodromy of the (φ,Γ)‑module at level n+1, and passing to the limit gives the full categorical equivalence. The mixed‑characteristic Prym p‑divisible group is a derived object whose classical truncation is the ordinary Prym variety; its Dieudonné module carries the action of the Hecke algebra, and the categorical trace formula identifies this action with the convolution product in the spherical Hecke category of Ĝ.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Framing and the Categorical Satake Equivalence

A framing of a G‑bundle at a point x ∈ X is a trivialization of the fibre, i.e. a reduction of the structure group to the trivial subgroup at x. The moduli stack BunG, fr of framed bundles is a derived stack whose cotangent complex at a point (E, φ) is the shifted Lie algebra 𝔤[−1] ⊕ H1(X, ad E). The categorical Satake equivalence for the affine Grassmannian GrG identifies the spherical Hecke category DG(O)(GrG) with the category Rep(Ĝ) of finite‑dimensional representations of the Langlands dual group. When a framing is present, the Hecke category is replaced by the category of G(O)‑equivariant D‑modules on GrG that are also equivariant with respect to the evaluation map at x. This category is equivalent to the category of modules over the quantum Hamiltonian reduction of the affine W‑algebra Wk(𝔤) at level k = −h∨ (critical level). The reduction is performed with respect to the nilpotent element corresponding to the framing; at critical level the quantum parameter q = 1 and the W‑algebra becomes the classical W‑algebra, whose representation category is equivalent to the category of D‑modules on the nilpotent cone of Ĝ. The categorical Hecke operators act on D(BunG, fr) by convolution with kernels supported on the framed Hecke correspondence. Under the categorical Satake equivalence, these operators correspond to the action of Rep(Ĝ) on the category of modules over the reduced W‑algebra. The Hecke eigensheaf condition for a framed bundle therefore says that the corresponding object is an eigenobject for the action of Rep(Ĝ) with eigenvalue given by a point of the oper space OpĜ(X) that is compatible with the framing. The categorical trace of the framed Hecke functors computes the character of the corresponding representation of Ĝ, and the resulting Verlinde‑type formula involves the equivariant K‑theory of the framed moduli space. In the Kapustin–Witten physical picture, the framed moduli space corresponds to a boundary condition of type (B, A, A) in the topologically twisted N = 4 super Yang–Mills theory, and the category of branes is precisely D(BunG, fr). The S‑duality of the gauge theory exchanges this boundary condition with a dual boundary condition on the Hitchin moduli space for Ĝ, realizing the categorical Satake equivalence as a physical duality of boundary conditions.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Level Structure and the Categorical Trace Formula

For a reductive group G over a curve X and a divisor D defining a level structure, the categorical trace of the Hecke functors on D(BunG, D) is defined as the Hochschild homology of the monoidal category of Hecke operators acting on the automorphic category. By the categorical geometric Langlands equivalence, this trace is identified with the Hochschild homology of the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the spectral side LocSysĜ, D. The latter computes the derived global sections of the structure sheaf on the derived loop space of LocSysĜ, D, which by the derived version of the Atiyah–Bott localization formula equals the cohomology of the moduli stack of Ĝ‑local systems with its natural shifted symplectic structure. The trace formula therefore yields an identity between the categorical trace of the automorphic Hecke category and the cohomological Hall algebra of the spectral curve. In the case G = GLn and D empty, this recovers the classical Verlinde formula for the dimension of spaces of conformal blocks. For non‑trivial level structure, the trace formula incorporates the contribution of the parahoric subgroups at D, and the result is a factorization of the Verlinde formula into a product of local factors associated to each point of D, each factor being the character of the corresponding representation of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at the prescribed level. The proof uses the factorization homology of the Ran space of X together with the excision property of Hochschild homology, reducing the global computation to a product of local traces on the affine Grassmannian. The local trace on the affine Grassmannian is computed by the geometric Satake equivalence and equals the character of the corresponding representation of the Langlands dual group. This categorical trace formula is the precise mathematical formulation of the statement that the geometric Langlands correspondence preserves the "spectral decomposition" of the automorphic category, and it provides a powerful tool for computing dimensions of spaces of Hecke eigensheaves in concrete examples.

  • Confusing the categorical Hecke category for a parahoric level structure with the spherical Hecke category; the former involves the affine flag variety, not the affine Grassmannian.
  • Assuming the Whittaker category for parabolic bundles is equivalent to the full D‑module category on the oper space without the non‑degenerate character twist.
  • Neglecting the derived nature of the mixed‑characteristic Prym p‑divisible group; its classical truncation loses the Hecke action.
  • Treating the framed Hecke category as an ordinary convolution category; it is a quantum Hamiltonian reduction of the affine W‑algebra at critical level.
  • Identifying the categorical trace of Hecke functors with the ordinary trace on K‑theory; the categorical trace lives in Hochschild homology and requires derived loop space techniques.
  • Overlooking the role of the Feigin–Frenkel center in controlling the central character of Hecke eigensheaves at critical level for non‑simply connected groups.
  • Believing the categorical Satake equivalence for the affine flag variety of a Levi factor is the same as the spherical equivalence; the former involves the full affine braid group action.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Twisted D‑modules, Gerbes, and the B‑Field in Geometric Langlands

The classical geometric Langlands correspondence relates D‑modules on the moduli stack BunG to quasi‑coherent sheaves on the moduli stack LocSysĜ. When a topological B‑field is turned on — mathematically a class in H2(BunG, 𝔾m) or, more generally, a 𝔾m‑gerbe — the categories on both sides must be twisted. On the automorphic side one replaces D(BunG) by the category of modules over the twisted sheaf of differential operators Dλ, where λ is a weight of G determining the gerbe. On the spectral side the structure sheaf of LocSysĜ is replaced by a twisted sheaf of categories, often described as a sheaf of A∞‑categories or a factorization algebra with a non‑trivial central charge. The Kapustin–Witten analysis shows that the B‑field corresponds to the discrete θ‑angle of the four‑dimensional N = 4 super Yang–Mills theory; S‑duality exchanges the B‑field for G with a dual B‑field for Ĝ determined by the Langlands dual weight λ∨. For simply connected G the gerbe on BunG is trivial, but for adjoint G it is classified by the fundamental group π1(G). The twisted geometric Satake equivalence identifies the spherical Hecke category for the twisted affine Grassmannian GrG,λ with the category of representations of the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) at a root of unity determined by λ. The categorical trace of the twisted Hecke functors computes the Verlinde formula for the twisted conformal blocks, and the resulting identity is the twisted version of the geometric Langlands correspondence. In the derived setting the twist is encoded by a shifted symplectic structure on the derived moduli stack, and the equivalence is an equivalence of E2‑categories. The case G = PGLn and λ a generator of H2(BunPGLn, 𝔾m) recovers the classical projective geometric Langlands correspondence studied by Laumon and Rothstein.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Bruhat–Tits Building and Parahoric Group Schemes in the Geometric Langlands Program

When the curve X is replaced by a formal disc Spec 𝒪 or a local field F, the moduli stack BunG is replaced by the affine flag variety FlG = G(F)/I where I is an Iwahori subgroup. The Bruhat–Tits building ℬ(G,F) parametrizes parahoric subgroups P ⊂ G(F) up to conjugacy; each vertex corresponds to a parahoric group scheme 𝒫 over 𝒪 whose generic fiber is G and whose special fiber is a connected reductive group over the residue field. The geometric Langlands correspondence with parahoric level structure at a point x ∈ X replaces BunG by the moduli stack BunG,𝒫 of G‑bundles with a reduction to 𝒫 at x. The Hecke operators are now defined using the affine flag variety Fl𝒫 = G(F)/𝒫(𝒪) instead of the affine Grassmannian. The categorical geometric Satake equivalence for parahoric groups, established by Zhu and others, identifies the category of 𝒫(𝒪)‑equivariant perverse sheaves on Fl𝒫 with the category of representations of the Langlands dual group Ĝ equipped with a nilpotent element determined by the parahoric. The spectral side becomes the moduli stack LocSysĜ,𝒫∨ of Ĝ‑local systems with a prescribed singularity type at x dual to 𝒫. The nearby cycles functor on the affine flag variety computes the monodromy of the Hecke eigensheaves and matches the weight filtration on the dual side. In the Kapustin–Witten picture the parahoric level structure corresponds to a boundary condition of type (B, A, A) supported on a Levi subgroup of the dual group, and S‑duality exchanges it with a dual boundary condition on the Hitchin moduli space. The categorical trace of the parahoric Hecke functors yields a factorization of the Verlinde formula into local factors indexed by the vertices of the Bruhat–Tits building, each factor being a character of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at the level determined by the parahoric.

Worked Example: Parahoric Hecke Category for GL(2) at an Iwahori Level
Let G = GL(2) over a local field F with ring of integers 𝒪 and uniformizer ϖ. Let I ⊂ GL(2,𝒪) be the standard Iwahori subgroup of matrices congruent to upper‑triangular modulo ϖ. Describe the category of I‑equivariant perverse sheaves on the affine flag variety Fl = GL(2,F)/I and its relation to the representation category of the dual group Ĝ = GL(2).
The affine flag variety Fl for GL(2) is an ind‑scheme whose connected components are indexed by the affine Weyl group Waff = W ⋉ X*(T) ≅ S2 ⋉ ℤ2. The I‑orbits are the Schubert cells Cw for w ∈ Waff. The category PervI(Fl) of I‑equivariant perverse sheaves is generated by the intersection cohomology complexes ICw of the Schubert varieties X̄w. The convolution product ⋆ makes this a monoidal category. The geometric Satake equivalence for the Iwahori (Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg) identifies PervI(Fl) with the category of modules over the affine Hecke algebra Haff of type Ã1. The affine Hecke algebra has generators Ts (simple reflection) and θλ (cocharacters) with relations Ts2 = (q−1)Ts + q and Tsθλ − θs(λ)Ts = (q−1)(θλ−θs(λ))/(1−θ−α). At q = 1 this degenerates to the group algebra of the extended affine Weyl group. The Langlands dual group is Ĝ = GL(2). The category Rep(GL(2)) embeds into PervI(Fl) via the spherical Hecke category (the subcategory of GL(2,𝒪)‑equivariant sheaves on the affine Grassmannian Gr = GL(2,F)/GL(2,𝒪)). The full Iwahori category is a categorical representation of Rep(GL(2)) via the convolution action of the spherical Hecke category. The simple objects ICw correspond to the Kazhdan–Lusztig basis of Haff, and their characters give the affine Kazhdan–Lusztig polynomials. The spectral side is the moduli stack of GL(2)‑local systems with an Iwahori‑type singularity, i.e., a local system on the punctured disc with monodromy in the Iwahori subgroup. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors computes the character of the corresponding affine Kac–Moody representation at level 1.
Exam Strategy: Parahoric vs. Spherical Hecke
Distinguish the spherical Hecke category (convolution on GrG = G(F)/G(𝒪)) from the parahoric/Iwahori Hecke category (convolution on Fl𝒫 = G(F)/𝒫(𝒪)). The former is monoidal and equivalent to Rep(Ĝ); the latter is a module category over the spherical one and is equivalent to modules over the affine Hecke algebra. In questions about level structure, check whether the level is "full" (parahoric = G(𝒪)) or "Iwahori" (Borel reduction). The categorical trace formula factors differently in each case.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Categorical Tannakian Reconstruction and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles

The Tannakian formalism reconstructs an affine group scheme from its category of representations equipped with a fiber functor. In the geometric Langlands program the automorphic category D(BunG) carries a natural action of the spherical Hecke category SatG ≃ Rep(Ĝ). This action makes D(BunG) a module category over Rep(Ĝ). The categorical Tannakian reconstruction theorem (due to Deligne, Milne, and in the derived setting to Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum) states that a symmetric monoidal category 𝒞 with a conservative fiber functor to vector spaces is equivalent to Rep(H) for a unique affine group scheme H. In the geometric Langlands context one considers the endomorphism category EndRep(Ĝ)(D(BunG)) of Rep(Ĝ)‑module endofunctors of the automorphic category. This endomorphism category is itself symmetric monoidal and admits a fiber functor given by evaluation at the trivial bundle. The reconstructed group scheme is precisely the Langlands dual group Ĝ (or a central extension thereof at critical level). Conversely, on the spectral side the category QCoh(LocSysĜ) is a module category over Rep(Ĝ) via tensor product, and its endomorphism category reconstructs G. The geometric Langlands equivalence is then an equivalence of Rep(Ĝ)‑module categories, which by Tannakian duality implies an equivalence of the reconstructed group schemes. At the critical level the fiber functor is replaced by a functor to the category of modules over the Feigin–Frenkel center, and the reconstructed object is the completed universal enveloping algebra of ĝ at critical level. The categorical trace of the Hecke action computes the Hochschild homology of the endomorphism category, which by the derived Tannakian formalism equals the cohomology of the derived loop space of LocSysĜ. This provides a conceptual proof of the categorical trace formula without relying on the geometric Satake equivalence pointwise.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Isomonodromic Deformations, Stokes Phenomena, and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for Irregular Connections

When the G‑local systems on X are allowed to have irregular singularities, the moduli stack LocSysG is replaced by the moduli stack of meromorphic connections with prescribed irregular type. The irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence (Deligne, Malgrange, Sabbah, Kedlaya) relates such connections to Stokes‑filtered local systems, i.e., local systems on a real blow‑up of X at the singular points equipped with a filtration by Stokes sectors. The geometric Langlands correspondence for irregular connections, formulated by Boalch and developed by Arinkin–Gaitsgory, replaces QCoh(LocSysĜ) by the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived moduli stack of Stokes‑filtered Ĝ‑local systems. On the automorphic side the Hecke operators are modified by the wild ramification data: the affine Grassmannian is replaced by the affine flag variety of the loop group with a prescribed irregular type, and the spherical Hecke category is replaced by the category of sheaves on the wild affine Grassmannian. The isomonodromic deformation equations (Schlesinger, Jimbo–Miwa–Ueno) describe the variation of the irregular connection with respect to the positions of the singularities and the Stokes data. These equations are Hamiltonian with respect to the natural symplectic structure on the wild character variety, and their quantization yields the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical level. The Stokes matrices provide the monodromy data for the Hecke eigensheaves, and the categorical trace of the wild Hecke functors computes the partition function of the corresponding irregular conformal blocks. In the Kapustin–Witten picture the irregular singularity corresponds to a surface defect of type (A, B, A) in the four‑dimensional theory, and S‑duality exchanges it with a dual defect on the Hitchin moduli space. The Stokes phenomenon manifests as wall‑crossing of the spectral networks on the Hitchin base, and the categorical equivalence respects the wall‑crossing formulas of Gaiotto–Moore–Neitzke.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Crystal Bases, Canonical Bases, and the Geometric Langlands Correspondence at Roots of Unity

The geometric Satake equivalence identifies the spherical Hecke category PervG(𝒪)(GrG) with Rep(Ĝ). When the quantum parameter q is specialized to a root of unity, the representation category of the quantum group Uq(ĝ) is no longer semisimple; its simple objects are parametrized by the alcoves of the affine Weyl group. Lusztig's canonical basis and Kashiwara's crystal basis provide a combinatorial framework for these representations. The geometric realization of the canonical basis uses the intersection cohomology of Schubert varieties in the affine Grassmannian (or the affine flag variety for the Iwahori case). The crystal basis is realized as the set of irreducible components of the Lusztig nilpotent variety or, equivalently, as the set of MV cycles in the affine Grassmannian (Mirković–Vilonen). In the geometric Langlands program at roots of unity, the automorphic category is replaced by the category of D‑modules on BunG with a central character corresponding to the root of unity. The Hecke eigensheaves are then objects in the derived category of the modular representation category of Ĝ. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence at roots of unity, conjectured by Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg and proved in parts by Gaitsgory–Raskin, relates this category to the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived moduli stack of Ĝ‑local systems with a Frobenius‑twisted structure. The Verlinde formula at roots of unity computes the dimensions of the spaces of conformal blocks for the affine Kac–Moody algebra at the corresponding level, and the categorical trace formula expresses these dimensions as the Euler characteristics of the corresponding Hecke eigensheaves. The crystal graph of the quantum group appears as the graph of Hecke modifications of the eigensheaves, and the canonical basis elements correspond to the intersection cohomology complexes of the Schubert varieties in the affine Grassmannian. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at roots of unity thus provides a geometric categorification of the modular representation theory of quantum groups.

  • Confusing the twisted D‑module category for a B‑field with the untwisted category; the twist changes the central character and the Hecke eigenvalues.
  • Assuming the parahoric Hecke category is monoidal; it is only a module category over the spherical Hecke category.
  • Neglecting the role of the Bruhat–Tits building in parametrizing parahoric level structures; each vertex gives a distinct geometric Langlands correspondence.
  • Treating the categorical Tannakian reconstruction as a formal tautology; the conservativity of the fiber functor is a deep theorem requiring the geometric Satake equivalence.
  • Overlooking the Stokes filtration in the irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence; the spectral side for wild ramification is not simply LocSysĜ but a stack of Stokes‑filtered local systems.
  • Identifying the crystal basis with the canonical basis at generic q; they coincide only after specialization to q = 0 and require the MV cycle construction.
  • Believing the quantum geometric Langlands at roots of unity is equivalent to the classical correspondence modulo p; the modular representation theory of quantum groups is distinct from the modular representation theory of algebraic groups.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Fixed Determinant and the Prym Variety in Mixed Characteristic

The extension of the geometric Langlands program to mixed characteristic replaces the base field by a complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero with perfect residue field of characteristic p. In this setting the moduli stack BunG of G‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X over the valuation ring is no longer a scheme but a derived Artin stack. The fixed‑determinant condition selects a connected component of BunG and the associated Prym variety appears as the fiber of the determinant morphism to the Jacobian of X. Recent work of Zhu and of Bhatt–Scholze constructs a categorical equivalence between the derived category of ℓ‑adic sheaves on the fixed‑determinant locus and the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived moduli of Langlands dual local systems, using the theory of perfectoid spaces and the Fargues–Fontaine curve. The Hecke operators are defined via modifications of G‑bundles at points of the special fiber, and their eigenvalues are encoded by the spectral data of the corresponding local system. The Prym variety enters as the abelian part of the Hitchin fibration in mixed characteristic, and its cohomology carries a natural action of the spherical Hecke algebra. The compatibility of this action with the geometric Satake equivalence in mixed characteristic is established by comparing the nearby cycles functor on the affine Grassmannian over the valuation ring with the classical Satake isomorphism over the residue field. This comparison yields a mixed‑characteristic analogue of the Ngô support theorem, which controls the decomposition of the direct image of the constant sheaf under the Hitchin map. The resulting categorical trace formula expresses the dimensions of spaces of automorphic forms in terms of the Euler characteristics of Hecke eigensheaves, now computed in the ℓ‑adic setting.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Level Structure and the Loop Group at Critical Level in Positive Characteristic

When the curve X is defined over an algebraically closed field of characteristic p > 0, the loop group LG acquires a Frobenius twist that interacts non‑trivially with the critical level of the affine Kac–Moody algebra. A level structure on a G‑bundle is a trivialization of its pullback along the Frobenius morphism, and the moduli stack of such bundles is a finite étale cover of BunG. The categorical geometric Langlands conjecture at critical level predicts an equivalence between the category of D‑modules on this level‑structured moduli stack and the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of LG‑local systems with a compatible Frobenius structure. The critical level is characterized by the vanishing of the Sugawara central charge, which in positive characteristic forces the center of the completed enveloping algebra to be isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of opers. The Feigin–Frenkel center thus becomes a commutative algebra object in the category of D‑modules, and its spectrum is the oper moduli space. The Hecke eigensheaves are then D‑modules equipped with an action of this center, and their eigenvalues are given by opers. The proof strategy of Gaitsgory–Raskin uses the geometric Satake equivalence in positive characteristic, which identifies the spherical Hecke category with the representation category of the Langlands dual group scheme over the field. The level structure introduces a parahoric subgroup of LG, and the corresponding Hecke category is a module category over the spherical Hecke category. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors computes the trace of Frobenius on the cohomology of the moduli stack, yielding a positive‑characteristic analogue of the Arthur–Selberg trace formula. The main technical input is the construction of the Whittaker category in positive characteristic and the verification of the geometric Casselman–Shalika formula, which identifies the Whittaker coefficient of a Hecke eigensheaf with the intersection cohomology of the corresponding Schubert variety in the affine Grassmannian.

Worked Example
Explain why the critical level in positive characteristic forces the center of the completed enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra to be isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of opers, and how this identification is used to construct Hecke eigensheaves.
At critical level the Sugawara construction yields a central element whose eigenvalue vanishes; in characteristic p this implies the Feigin–Frenkel center maps isomorphically onto the algebra of functions on the oper moduli space. A Hecke eigensheaf is a D‑module on the level‑structured BunG equipped with a homomorphism from this center to its endomorphism algebra. The eigenvalue of the center is then an oper, and the geometric Casselman–Shalika formula identifies the Whittaker model of the eigensheaf with the intersection cohomology of the corresponding Schubert cell, providing the required Hecke eigenvalue property.
Exam Strategy
When a question asks for the role of the critical level, state the vanishing of the Sugawara central charge, the resulting isomorphism of the Feigin–Frenkel center with functions on opers, and the consequent parametrization of Hecke eigensheaves by opers. Cite the Gaitsgory–Raskin proof outline and the geometric Casselman–Shalika formula as the key technical tools.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Framing and the Geometric Satake Equivalence for Non‑Simply Connected Groups in Positive Characteristic

A framing of a G‑bundle on a curve X is a trivialization of its restriction to a fixed point x ∈ X. The moduli stack of framed bundles is a torsor over BunG under the loop group LG, and the geometric Satake equivalence identifies the category of perverse sheaves on the affine Grassmannian GrG with the representation category of the Langlands dual group Ĝ. For non‑simply connected groups the affine Grassmannian has several connected components indexed by the fundamental group π₁(G). In positive characteristic the Satake equivalence must be formulated for the group scheme Ĝ over the field, and the component group acts by tensor autoequivalences on the spherical Hecke category. The framed moduli stack decomposes accordingly, and the Hecke operators preserve the component decomposition. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence for framed bundles then relates the category of D‑modules on the framed moduli stack to the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of framed Ĝ‑local systems. The framing condition on the spectral side corresponds to a choice of a fiber functor on the category of representations of Ĝ, which is equivalent to a point of the Langlands dual group. The compatibility of the equivalence with the component group action is proved by analyzing the monodromy of the nearby cycles functor on the affine Grassmannian over the valuation ring and its reduction modulo p. This analysis uses the theory of ℓ‑adic sheaves on the affine Grassmannian and the decomposition theorem in positive characteristic, as developed by Zhu. The resulting equivalence respects the tensor structure on both sides and provides a categorification of the classical Satake isomorphism for non‑simply connected groups in positive characteristic.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with Wild Ramification and Irregular Singularities via Spectral Networks in Mixed Characteristic

Wild ramification corresponds to allowing irregular singularities in the connection on the spectral side. In mixed characteristic the irregular Riemann–Hilbert correspondence is replaced by the theory of (φ, Γ)‑modules over the Robba ring, and the Stokes data are encoded by a filtration on the associated filtered (φ, N)‑module. The moduli stack of wildly ramified G‑local systems is a derived Artin stack equipped with a natural symplectic structure coming from the Atiyah–Bott form. Spectral networks, introduced by Gaiotto–Moore–Neitzke, provide a combinatorial description of the wall‑crossing of the BPS spectrum on the Hitchin base. In mixed characteristic the Hitchin base is a scheme over the valuation ring, and the spectral network varies with the parameter. The categorical geometric Langlands equivalence for wild ramification is expected to intertwine the category of D‑modules on the moduli of wildly ramified G‑bundles with the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of wildly ramified Ĝ‑local systems, compatibly with the wall‑crossing functors. The construction of the Hecke eigensheaves in this setting uses the theory of irregular conformal blocks, which are defined as the cohomology of the moduli stack of irregular connections with coefficients in a suitable line bundle. The categorical trace of the wild Hecke functors computes the partition function of these irregular conformal blocks, and the Verlinde formula in mixed characteristic expresses this partition function in terms of the Euler characteristics of the corresponding eigensheaves. The main technical challenge is to extend the Ngô support theorem to the wild Hitchin fibration, which requires a detailed analysis of the singularities of the wild Hitchin fibers and their nearby cycles.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Parabolic Structure and the Levi Subgroup in Positive Characteristic

A parabolic structure on a G‑bundle is a reduction of its structure group to a parabolic subgroup P at a finite set of points, together with a choice of a weight in the cocharacter lattice of the Levi quotient L = P/U. The moduli stack of parabolic bundles is a smooth Artin stack fibered over BunG with fibers isomorphic to the flag variety G/P. In positive characteristic the geometric Langlands correspondence for parabolic bundles is formulated by replacing the automorphic category with the category of D‑modules on the parabolic moduli stack that are equivariant with respect to the action of the unipotent radical U. The spectral side is the derived stack of Ĝ‑local systems equipped with a reduction to the Langlands dual Levi subgroup ˇL at the same points, together with a compatible monodromy weight. The Hecke operators act by modifying the parabolic structure, and their eigenvalues are given by the corresponding ˇL‑local systems. The induction and restriction functors between parabolic categories for different parabolics are realized geometrically by the correspondences of partial flag varieties, and they satisfy the expected adjunction properties. The categorical trace formula for parabolic Hecke operators computes the trace of Frobenius on the cohomology of the parabolic moduli stack, yielding a positive‑characteristic analogue of the Arthur trace formula for Levi subgroups. The proof of the equivalence uses the geometric Satake equivalence for the Levi subgroup, which identifies the spherical Hecke category of L with the representation category of ˇL, and the compatibility of the induction functor with the tensor product on the spectral side. The main input is the decomposition theorem for the map from the parabolic moduli stack to the Levi moduli stack, which holds in positive characteristic by the work of Bhatt–Scholze on the p‑adic Simpson correspondence.

  • Confusing the mixed‑characteristic Prym variety with its equal‑characteristic analogue; the nearby cycles functor introduces a monodromy operator absent in characteristic zero.
  • Assuming the critical level center is unchanged in positive characteristic; the Frobenius twist modifies the Feigin–Frenkel isomorphism and forces the oper condition.
  • Neglecting the component group action on the spherical Hecke category for non‑simply connected groups; it is essential for the framed equivalence.
  • Treating wild ramification in mixed characteristic as a straightforward extension of the tame case; the (φ, Γ)‑module formalism and Stokes filtrations are fundamentally different.
  • Overlooking the role of the unipotent radical in the parabolic automorphic category; equivariance under U is required for the correct Hecke eigenvalues.
  • Believing the Ngô support theorem holds verbatim for the wild Hitchin fibration; the singularities of wild fibers demand a refined nearby cycles analysis.
  • Each of these traps reflects a subtle interaction between geometry and arithmetic that must be mastered for advanced examinations.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Categorical Local Systems for Higher Rank Groups

The derived stack of categorical local systems, denoted LocSysĜ(X), classifies Ĝ‑local systems on a smooth projective curve X together with higher homotopical data. For higher rank groups the stack is no longer a classical scheme but a derived Artin stack whose cotangent complex encodes the deformation theory of irregular connections. The categorical geometric Langlands conjecture predicts an equivalence between the DG‑category of D‑modules on BunG(X) and the DG‑category of ind‑coherent sheaves on LocSysĜ(X). In the higher rank setting the Hecke eigensheaf condition is formulated by requiring that the Hecke functors act via tensor product with the universal local system on the spectral side. The proof for GL(n) uses the geometric Satake equivalence for the affine Grassmannian GrG and the factorization structure of the Hecke category. For general reductive G the argument proceeds by reduction to the simply connected cover and by analyzing the component group π₁(G) which acts on the spherical Hecke category. The categorical local systems framework also incorporates the Riemann–Hilbert correspondence for irregular singularities, where the Stokes data are encoded as enhancements of the local system. This enhancement is essential for the compatibility with the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical level.

Recent work of Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum and of Arinkin–Gaitsgory constructs the categorical trace of the Hecke functors and identifies it with the factorization homology of the universal local system on the Ran space of X. The trace formula yields a categorified version of the Arthur–Selberg trace formula, where orbital integrals are replaced by categorical characters of the loop group. The higher rank case introduces non‑abelian gerbes on the Hitchin base, and the support theorem of Ngô is extended to the derived setting using the theory of perverse schobers. These developments show that the categorical local systems perspective unifies the classical geometric Langlands correspondence, the quantum version, and the categorical trace formalism.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Duality for Quantum K‑Theory

Quantum K‑theory provides a K‑theoretic lift of the quantum cohomology ring of the moduli space of G‑bundles. The elliptic stable envelopes of Okounkov and Maulik–Okounkov define a basis in the equivariant K‑theory of the Nakajima quiver varieties that appear as Coulomb branches of three‑dimensional N=4 gauge theories. These envelopes satisfy a wall‑crossing formula governed by the quantum K‑theoretic R‑matrix, which coincides with the monodromy of the quantum difference equation associated to the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence. The K‑theoretic Hall algebra of Schiffmann–Vasserot, and its elliptic generalization by Feigin–Tsymbaliuk, acts on the K‑theory of the moduli of bundles and provides a representation of the quantum toroidal algebra of type A. Under the geometric Langlands duality this action is transported to the action of the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) on the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on LocSysĜ(X). The parameter q is identified with the exponentiated Planck constant of the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence, and the critical level corresponds to q a root of unity.

The duality for quantum K‑theory is formulated as an equivalence of monoidal DG‑categories between the spherical Hecke category in quantum K‑theory and the category of representations of the quantum Langlands dual group at the corresponding quantum parameter. The proof uses the geometric Satake equivalence in K‑theory, established by Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima, and the identification of the quantum K‑theoretic convolution product with the fusion product of representations. The elliptic stable envelopes provide the explicit intertwiner between the two sides, and their wall‑crossing matches the braiding in the quantum group category. This framework also explains the appearance of the elliptic quantum group in the study of the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence for torus knots and links, where the categorified Jones polynomial is realized as the categorical trace of the corresponding Hecke functor.

Examiner's trap
A question asks: "Explain why the quantum K‑theoretic R‑matrix coincides with the monodromy of the quantum difference equation in the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence." The trap is to confuse the quantum cohomology R‑matrix with the quantum K‑theory R‑matrix; the latter depends on the equivariant parameter ℏ and the K‑theoretic Novikov variable, while the former depends only on the quantum cohomology parameter.
The quantum K‑theory R‑matrix is defined by the elliptic stable envelope construction, which solves the quantum difference equation ∇qΨ = 0 where ∇q = qℏ∂/∂ℏ − A(ℏ) and A(ℏ) is the quantum connection in K‑theory. The monodromy of this q‑difference equation around ℏ = 0 is precisely the R‑matrix because the stable envelopes provide a fundamental solution matrix whose analytic continuation yields the braiding. In quantum cohomology the corresponding equation is a differential equation, and its monodromy gives the cohomological R‑matrix; the two coincide only after taking the classical limit ℏ → 0 and identifying the Novikov variable with the exponentiated Kähler parameter.
Exam‑day strategy
When a question mixes quantum cohomology and quantum K‑theory, first identify which deformation parameter (ℏ or q) is present. Write down the defining equation (differential vs. q‑difference) and the associated monodromy. This separates the two R‑matrices and avoids the common conflation.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Fixed Determinant and the Prym Variety in Mixed Characteristic

Fix a reductive group G over a mixed‑characteristic valuation ring O with residue field k of characteristic p and fraction field K of characteristic zero. The moduli stack BunGdet=δ(X) of G‑bundles with fixed determinant δ is a union of connected components indexed by the fundamental group π₁(G). Over the generic fiber the Prym variety Prym(X,δ) parametrizes line bundles on a spectral cover with norm δ; over the special fiber the same construction yields a p‑divisible group whose Dieudonné module is computed by the nearby cycles complex of the Hitchin fibration. The geometric Langlands equivalence in mixed characteristic is expected to intertwine the category of D‑modules on BunGdet=δ(X) with the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of Ĝ‑local systems with prescribed determinant, compatibly with the nearby cycles functor Ψη. The nearby cycles introduce a monodromy operator N on the special fiber, which corresponds under the equivalence to the action of the Arthur SL₂ on the spectral side. The Feigin–Frenkel center at critical level is deformed by the Frobenius twist, and the oper condition becomes a condition on the associated (φ,Γ)‑module.

The proof of the mixed‑characteristic Prym duality uses the p‑adic Simpson correspondence of Bhatt–Scholze, which identifies the category of Higgs bundles on the special fiber with the category of (φ,Γ)‑modules on the generic fiber. The Ngô support theorem is extended to the mixed‑characteristic Hitchin fibration by analyzing the singularities of the fibers using the theory of log‑smooth morphisms and the weight‑monodromy conjecture. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors computes the trace of Frobenius on the cohomology of the Prym variety, yielding a mixed‑characteristic analogue of the Arthur trace formula. The main technical input is the compatibility of the geometric Satake equivalence with the nearby cycles functor, which is established by the work of Zhu on the affine Grassmannian over mixed‑characteristic bases.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Level Structure and the Loop Group at Mixed Level

A level structure on a G‑bundle is a trivialization of its pullback to a formal neighborhood of a divisor D ⊂ X. The moduli stack BunGlevel(X) carries an action of the loop group L+G, and the geometric Langlands correspondence with level structure is formulated as an equivalence between the category of D‑modules on BunGlevel(X) equivariant under the pro‑unipotent radical of L+G and the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of Ĝ‑local systems with a compatible level structure on the Langlands dual side. At mixed level the level structure is allowed to vary between critical and non‑critical values along the components of D. The critical level corresponds to the Feigin–Frenkel center acting centrally on the automorphic category, while the non‑critical level introduces a non‑trivial central character given by the evaluation of the quantum Hamiltonian at the level parameter. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at mixed level is governed by a family of quantum groups Uq(Ĝ) where q varies with the level.

The construction of the equivalence uses the geometric Satake equivalence for the affine flag variety FlG with parahoric level structure, developed by Zhu and by Finkelberg–Mirković. The Hecke operators at mixed level are defined by correspondences of moduli stacks with varying level structures, and their composition is controlled by the convolution product in the spherical Hecke category at the corresponding level. The categorical trace of these Hecke functors computes the partition function of the associated quantum K‑theoretic gauge theory, and the Verlinde formula at mixed level expresses this partition function in terms of the dimensions of spaces of conformal blocks for the quantum group at the appropriate root of unity. The main difficulty is the compatibility of the nearby cycles functor with the change of level, which is resolved by the theory of factorization algebras on the Ran space and the factorization homology of the quantum group.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Parabolic Structure and the Levi Subgroup in Positive Characteristic

Let P ⊂ G be a parabolic subgroup with Levi quotient L = P/U. A parabolic G‑bundle is a G‑bundle equipped with a reduction of structure group to P at a finite set of points together with a dominant coweight λ ∈ X∗(L). The moduli stack BunG,P,λ(X) is smooth and fibers over BunG(X) with fiber the flag variety G/P. In positive characteristic the automorphic category is taken to be the category of D‑modules on BunG,P,λ(X) that are equivariant under the action of the unipotent radical U. The spectral side is the derived stack of Ĝ‑local systems equipped with a reduction to the Langlands dual Levi subgroup ˇL at the same points and a monodromy weight in the cocharacter lattice of ˇL. The Hecke operators modify the parabolic structure by changing the reduction and the weight, and their eigenvalues are given by the corresponding ˇL‑local systems.

The induction and restriction functors between parabolic categories for different parabolics are realized geometrically by the correspondences of partial flag varieties G/P ← G/(P∩Q) → G/Q. These functors satisfy the expected adjunction properties and are compatible with the tensor product on the spectral side via the geometric Satake equivalence for the Levi subgroup L. The decomposition theorem for the map BunG,P,λ(X) → BunL,λ(X) holds in positive characteristic by the work of Bhatt–Scholze on the p‑adic Simpson correspondence, which identifies the category of Higgs bundles with the category of (φ,Γ)‑modules. The categorical trace formula for parabolic Hecke operators computes the trace of Frobenius on the cohomology of the parabolic moduli stack, yielding a positive‑characteristic analogue of the Arthur trace formula for Levi subgroups. The main input is the compatibility of the geometric Satake equivalence for L with the induction functor, which follows from the factorization property of the Hecke category and the monoidality of the Satake equivalence.

  • Confusing the mixed‑characteristic Prym variety with its equal‑characteristic analogue; the nearby cycles functor introduces a monodromy operator absent in characteristic zero.
  • Assuming the critical level center is unchanged in positive characteristic; the Frobenius twist modifies the Feigin–Frenkel isomorphism and forces the oper condition.
  • Neglecting the component group action on the spherical Hecke category for non‑simply connected groups; it is essential for the framed equivalence.
  • Treating wild ramification in mixed characteristic as a straightforward extension of the tame case; the (φ,Γ)‑module formalism and Stokes filtrations are fundamentally different.
  • Overlooking the role of the unipotent radical in the parabolic automorphic category; equivariance under U is required for the correct Hecke eigenvalues.
  • Believing the Ngô support theorem holds verbatim for the wild Hitchin fibration; the singularities of wild fibers demand a refined nearby cycles analysis.
  • Assuming the quantum K‑theoretic R‑matrix coincides with the quantum cohomology R‑matrix without accounting for the equivariant parameter ℏ and the K‑theoretic Novikov variable.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Fixed Determinant and the Prym Variety in Mixed Characteristic

The mixed‑characteristic setting introduces a base scheme Spec ℤₚ and a special fibre over 𝔽ₚ. For a split reductive group G over ℤₚ, the moduli stack BunG(X) of G‑bundles on a smooth projective curve X/ℤₚ carries a natural determinant line bundle det : BunG → Picd(X). Fixing a degree d and a line bundle ℒ of that degree yields the fibre BunG,ℒ(X). When G = GLn the determinant condition is equivalent to fixing the top exterior power; for a general G one uses the abelianisation map G → G/[G,G] and pulls back a chosen character. The Prym variety appears as the kernel of the norm map Nm : Picd(X̃) → Picd(X) for a double cover X̃ → X, and it parametrises line bundles on X̃ with trivial norm. In mixed characteristic the Prym is a p‑divisible group over ℤₚ whose generic fibre is an abelian variety and whose special fibre is a semi‑abelian variety with toric part governed by the monodromy of the cover. The geometric Langlands functor is expected to send a Hecke eigensheaf on BunG,ℒ to a Ĝ‑local system on X whose determinant matches ℒ under the Langlands dual abelianisation. The main technical input is the p‑adic Simpson correspondence of Bhatt–Scholze, which identifies the category of Higgs bundles on the special fibre with (φ,Γ)‑modules on the generic fibre. This identification transports the Hecke action to a Frobenius‑equivariant action on the spectral side, and the nearby cycles functor on the Hitchin fibration produces the monodromy operator that distinguishes the mixed‑characteristic Prym from its equal‑characteristic counterpart. The resulting equivalence is compatible with the geometric Satake equivalence for the affine Grassmannian over ℤₚ, and the categorical trace computes the local factor of the L‑function of the associated automorphic representation.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence with Level Structure and the Loop Group at Non‑Critical Level

Let G be a split reductive group over a field k of characteristic zero and let X be a smooth projective curve. A level structure of level N ≥ 1 is a trivialisation of the G‑bundle on the formal neighbourhood of a reduced effective divisor D = ∑ xi of degree N. The moduli stack BunG(X,D) of G‑bundles with level structure is a smooth Deligne–Mumford stack equipped with a natural action of the loop group LG = G(k((t))) at each point of D. The automorphic category is taken to be the derived category of D‑modules on BunG(X,D) that are equivariant under the positive loop group L+G. On the spectral side one considers the derived stack LocSysĜ(X,D) of Ĝ‑local systems with prescribed monodromy at D; the monodromy data are encoded by a conjugacy class in the Langlands dual group Ĝ. The Hecke operators are defined by correspondences that modify the level structure at a point away from D, and their composition is governed by the convolution product in the spherical Hecke category SatG = D(L+GLG/L+G). At non‑critical level the centre of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at level κ acts by scalars determined by κ, and the Feigin–Frenkel centre is replaced by a smaller commutative algebra generated by the quantum Hamiltonians of the Gaudin model. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at non‑critical level is then an equivalence between the automorphic category and the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on LocSysĜ(X,D) twisted by a line bundle determined by κ. The proof uses the factorisation homology of the chiral algebra attached to the affine Kac–Moody algebra at level κ, together with the geometric Satake equivalence for the affine flag variety with parahoric level structure developed by Zhu and by Finkelberg–Mirković. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors reproduces the partition function of the associated 4‑dimensional N = 4 super Yang–Mills theory with surface defects, and the Verlinde formula expresses this partition function in terms of the dimensions of spaces of conformal blocks for the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) with q = exp(πi/κ).

Worked Example: Hecke Eigenvalue Computation for GL₂ with Level‑1 Structure
Let X be a smooth projective curve over ℂ and let G = GL₂. Consider the moduli stack BunGL₂(X, x) of rank‑2 vector bundles with a full level structure at a point x ∈ X. Let ℱ be a Hecke eigensheaf on this stack with eigenvalue a rank‑2 local system ℰ on X{x} having unipotent monodromy at x. Compute the eigenvalue of the spherical Hecke operator Hx corresponding to the fundamental coweight ω₁ on ℱ.
The spherical Hecke operator Hx acts by the correspondence that modifies the bundle at x by an elementary transformation of type ω₁. Under the geometric Satake equivalence, the fundamental representation V(ω₁) of the dual group Ĝ = GL₂ corresponds to the standard representation of GL₂. The eigenvalue of Hx on a Hecke eigensheaf is given by the fibre of the associated local system ℰ at x, i.e. the 2 × 2 matrix of monodromy. Since ℰ has unipotent monodromy at x, its local monodromy matrix is conjugate to (1 1; 0 1). The trace of this matrix is 2, which equals the eigenvalue of the Hecke operator on the rank‑1 summand of ℱ. More precisely, the Hecke functor sends ℱ to ℱ ⊠ V(ω₁) evaluated on ℰ, yielding ℱ ⊗ ℰx. Hence the eigenvalue is the vector space ℰx equipped with the unipotent monodromy action. In the Grothendieck group this gives the class [ℰx] = 2 · [trivial] + [non‑trivial Jordan block]. Therefore the Hecke eigenvalue is the 2‑dimensional representation of the inertia group at x with a single Jordan block of size 2.
Exam‑Day Strategy
When a question asks for the Hecke eigenvalue of a specific coweight, first identify the corresponding representation of the Langlands dual group via the geometric Satake equivalence. Then read off the local monodromy of the spectral local system at the relevant point. The eigenvalue is the fibre of that local system equipped with the monodromy action; its character is the trace of the monodromy matrix. Practice this translation on GL₂ and GL₃ examples to avoid confusing the coweight with the weight of the dual representation.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Framing and the Geometric Satake Equivalence for Non‑Simply Connected Groups

A framing of a G‑bundle at a point x ∈ X is a trivialisation of the fibre over x, i.e. a section of the associated G‑torsor over the formal disc. The moduli stack BunG(X, x) of framed G‑bundles is a G‑torsor over the usual moduli stack BunG(X). When G is not simply connected, the centre Z(G) acts non‑trivially on the framing, and the component group π₁(G) = X*(T)/Q∨ appears as the group of connected components of the affine Grassmannian GrG. The geometric Satake equivalence for non‑simply connected groups, proved by Zhu and by Richarz–Scholbach, identifies the spherical Hecke category SatG with the category of representations of the Langlands dual group Ĝ equipped with a grading by the component group π₁(G). Concretely, an object of SatG corresponds to a pair (V, χ) where V is a representation of Ĝ and χ is a character of π₁(G) describing the monodromy of the framing. The Hecke operators on the framed moduli stack are defined by correspondences that modify the framing together with the bundle; their composition is controlled by the convolution product in SatG which now carries the extra grading. On the spectral side, a framed G‑bundle corresponds to a Ĝ‑local system together with a reduction of structure group to the centraliser of the image of π₁(G) in Ĝ. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at critical level incorporates this data by twisting the ind‑coherent sheaves on LocSysĜ by a line bundle whose first Chern class is the image of the framing character under the natural map π₁(G) → H²(LocSysĜ, ℤ). The categorical trace of the framed Hecke functors computes the partition function of the 4‑dimensional N = 4 theory with a surface operator labelled by the framing character, and the Verlinde formula expresses this partition function in terms of the dimensions of spaces of conformal blocks for the affine Kac–Moody algebra at critical level with a prescribed weight for the centre.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence with Parahoric Structure and the Affine Flag Variety

Let G be a split reductive group over a field k and let x ∈ X be a point. A parahoric subgroup P ⊂ G(k[[t]]) is the stabiliser of a facet in the Bruhat–Tits building of G(k((t))). The moduli stack BunG,P(X) of G‑bundles with a reduction to P at x is a smooth Artin stack that fibres over BunG(X) with fibre the partial flag variety G/P. The automorphic category is the derived category of D‑modules on BunG,P(X) equivariant under the unipotent radical of P. The affine flag variety FlG = LG/P carries a natural action of the loop group LG, and the geometric Satake equivalence for the affine flag variety, due to Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg–Mirković, identifies the category of P‑equivariant perverse sheaves on FlG with the category of representations of the Langlands dual group Ĝ equipped with a compatible action of the dual parahoric subgroup ˇP. Hecke operators with parahoric level structure are defined by correspondences that modify the P‑reduction at x; their composition is governed by the convolution product in the parahoric Hecke category. At critical level the centre of the completed universal enveloping algebra acts through the Feigin–Frenkel centre, and the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence identifies the automorphic category with the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of Ĝ‑local systems with a prescribed ˇP‑reduction at x. The nearby cycles functor on the parahoric Hitchin fibration produces a monodromy operator that encodes the wild ramification data when the parahoric is not hyperspecial. The categorical trace of the parahoric Hecke functors yields the partition function of the 4‑dimensional theory with a surface defect labelled by the parahoric, and the Verlinde formula expresses this partition function in terms of the dimensions of spaces of conformal blocks for the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) with q a root of unity determined by the level and the parahoric type.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Fixed Determinant and the Prym Variety in Characteristic Zero

Over ℂ the Prym variety associated to a double cover π : X̃ → X is an abelian subvariety of the Jacobian Jac(X̃) defined as the kernel of the norm map Nm : Jac(X̃) → Jac(X). For G = GLn the condition of fixed determinant ℒ on X translates, via the spectral curve construction of the Hitchin fibration, to a condition on the spectral line bundle on X̃: its norm must equal ℒ twisted by a power of the canonical bundle. The moduli stack BunGLn,ℒ(X) of rank‑n bundles with determinant ℒ is a connected component of the full moduli stack, and its Hitchin fibre over a generic point is a torsor under the Prym variety of the corresponding spectral cover. The geometric Langlands functor sends a Hecke eigensheaf on BunGLn,ℒ to a GLn‑local system on X whose determinant is the rank‑1 local system corresponding to ℒ under the abelian geometric Langlands correspondence. The Hecke operators preserve the determinant condition because the spherical Hecke category for GLn is graded by the coweight lattice, and the determinant corresponds to the sum of fundamental coweights. The categorical trace of the Hecke functors on the fixed‑determinant automorphic category computes the partition function of the 4‑dimensional N = 4 theory with a topological defect that fixes the determinant line bundle; this partition function equals the sum over points of the Hitchin fibre of the exponential of the symplectic volume, which by the Duistermaat–Heckman formula reduces to the volume of the Prym variety. The Verlinde formula for the fixed‑determinant case expresses the dimension of the space of conformal blocks of the affine Kac–Moody algebra at level k with fixed central charge in terms of the number of points of the Prym variety over a finite field, confirming the compatibility of the geometric Langlands correspondence with the arithmetic Langlands programme.

Common Traps

  • Confusing the mixed‑characteristic Prym variety with its equal‑characteristic analogue; the nearby cycles functor introduces a monodromy operator absent in characteristic zero.
  • Assuming the critical level centre is unchanged in positive characteristic; the Frobenius twist modifies the Feigin–Frenkel isomorphism and forces the oper condition.
  • Neglecting the component group action on the spherical Hecke category for non‑simply connected groups; it is essential for the framed equivalence.
  • Treating wild ramification in mixed characteristic as a straightforward extension of the tame case; the (φ,Γ)‑module formalism and Stokes filtrations are fundamentally different.
  • Overlooking the role of the unipotent radical in the parabolic automorphic category; equivariance under U is required for the correct Hecke eigenvalues.
  • Believing the Ngô support theorem holds verbatim for the wild Hitchin fibration; the singularities of wild fibers demand a refined nearby cycles analysis.
  • Assuming the quantum K‑theoretic R‑matrix coincides with the quantum cohomology R‑matrix without accounting for the equivariant parameter ℏ and the K‑theoretic Novikov variable.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence for Mixed Characteristic Prym Varieties and the Nearby Cycles Monodromy

When the base curve is defined over a mixed‑characteristic ring such as the ring of Witt vectors W(k) for a perfect field k of characteristic p > 0, the Hitchin fibration acquires a relative version whose generic fibre lives in characteristic zero and whose special fibre lives in characteristic p. The Prym variety associated to a double cover π : X̃ → X is then a p-divisible group over W(k) whose generic fibre is an abelian variety and whose special fibre is a semi‑abelian variety with a unipotent radical. The nearby cycles functor Ψ on the relative Hitchin fibration produces a monodromy operator N on the cohomology of the Prym variety; this operator encodes the wild ramification data that appears when the parahoric level structure is not hyperspecial. In the work of Zhu and of Bouthier–Kazhdan–Varshavsky, the monodromy weight filtration on the nearby cycles complex is identified with the filtration by the depth of the Swan conductor of the corresponding Ĝ-local system. The geometric Langlands functor in this setting sends a Hecke eigensheaf on the moduli stack BunG,ℒ(X) of bundles with fixed determinant to a Ĝ-local system whose restriction to the inertia group at the ramification point is governed by the monodromy operator N. The compatibility with the arithmetic Langlands programme is expressed by the equality of the categorical trace of the Hecke functors on the mixed‑characteristic automorphic category with the trace of Frobenius on the nearby cycles complex, a statement that generalises the Grothendieck–Lefschetz trace formula to the categorical setting.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of Categorical Hecke Operators for Non‑Simply Connected Groups

For a reductive group G that is not simply connected, the spherical Hecke category SphG carries an action of the component group π0(G) ≅ Z(Ĝ)Γ, where Ĝ is the Langlands dual group and Γ is the absolute Galois group of the base field. This action is realised geometrically by the convolution product with the fundamental class of the connected components of the affine Grassmannian GrG. The categorical Hecke operators are then functors Hλ : D-mod(BunG) → D-mod(BunG) indexed by dominant coweights λ, but their composition is twisted by the component group action: Hλ ⋆ Hμ ≅ ⊕ν Hν ⊗ Vλ,μν where the multiplicity spaces carry a representation of π0(G). In the work of Gaitsgory–Lurie and of Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima, the categorical geometric Langlands equivalence is upgraded to an equivalence of π0(G)-equivariant categories, and the Hecke eigensheaf condition is required to be compatible with this equivariance. The resulting spectral side is the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack LocSysĜ(X) equipped with a π0(G)-action induced by the outer automorphisms of Ĝ. This refinement is essential for the geometric Langlands correspondence for groups such as PGLn or SO2n+1, where the centre of Ĝ is non‑trivial and the component group acts non‑trivially on the spherical Hecke category.

Worked Example: Component Group Action on Hecke Operators for PGL2
Let G = PGL2 over ℂ. The Langlands dual group is Ĝ = SL2. The component group π0(G) ≅ ℤ/2 acts on the affine Grassmannian GrPGL2 by the non‑trivial diagram automorphism of SL2. Compute the convolution Hω ⋆ Hω where ω is the fundamental coweight, and describe the π0(G)-action on the resulting multiplicity space.
The spherical Hecke category for PGL2 is equivalent to the category of representations of SL2 with a compatible action of the diagram automorphism σ (the non‑trivial element of π0). The fundamental coweight ω corresponds to the standard representation V of SL2. The convolution Hω ⋆ Hω corresponds to the tensor product V ⊗ V ≅ Sym2V ⊕ ∧2V. The diagram automorphism σ acts on V by the outer automorphism of SL2, which is trivial on the Lie algebra but exchanges the two weight spaces. On Sym2V the action is trivial, while on the one‑dimensional ∧2V it acts by multiplication by −1. Hence the multiplicity space for the trivial coweight (the determinant) carries the sign representation of π0(G), and the multiplicity space for the coweight 2ω carries the trivial representation. This matches the geometric description: the component group acts on the fibres of the Hitchin fibration by the monodromy of the spectral curve, and the sign appears precisely on the component corresponding to the non‑trivial element of the Prym variety.
Exam‑Day Strategy
When a question asks for the effect of the component group on Hecke operators, first identify the dual group and its centre. Write down the component group as the Galois‑invariant part of the centre. Then recall that the spherical Hecke category is the representation category of the dual group with the induced action. Convolution corresponds to tensor product of representations; decompose the tensor product and read off the action of the component group on each summand. This algorithm works uniformly for all non‑simply connected groups.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Parabolic Structure at Critical Level
