At critical level k = −h∨ the centre of the completed universal enveloping algebra of the affine Kac–Moody algebra ĝ is isomorphic to the algebra of functions on the space of Ĝ-opers on the curve X (the Feigin–Frenkel isomorphism). When a parabolic structure is imposed at a point x ∈ X with a fixed Levi subgroup L ⊂ G, the moduli stack BunG,L(X) of G-bundles with a reduction to L at x carries a natural action of the parabolic Hecke category. The quantum geometric Langlands correspondence at critical level identifies the category of D-modules on BunG,L with the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of Ĝ-local systems equipped with a compatible ˇL-reduction at x, where ˇL is the Langlands dual Levi. The oper condition on the spectral side translates into the requirement that the Ĝ-local system has a regular singularity at x with residue lying in the nilpotent cone of ˇl. The nearby cycles functor on the parabolic Hitchin fibration produces a monodromy operator that matches the Stokes data of the irregular oper when the parabolic weight is non‑generic. This compatibility was established by Frenkel–Gross and by Gaitsgory–Lurie using the theory of chiral algebras and factorization homology.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Framing and the Geometric Satake Equivalence at Non‑Critical Level

A framing of a G-bundle at a point x is a trivialisation of the fibre, which is equivalent to a section of the associated G-torsor over the formal disc Spec ℂ[[t]]. The moduli stack BunG,fr(X) of framed bundles is a G-torsor over BunG(X) and carries a natural action of the loop group G((t)). The geometric Satake equivalence for the affine Grassmannian GrG identifies the spherical Hecke category with the representation category of the Langlands dual group Ĝ. At non‑critical level k ≠ −h∨ the quantum geometric Langlands correspondence is expected to be an equivalence between the category of D-modules on BunG,fr twisted by the level k and the category of modules over the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) with q = exp(2πi/(k+h∨)). The framing provides a trivialisation of the determinant line bundle on the moduli stack, which allows one to define the quantum Hecke operators as functors that act by convolution with the quantum Satake category. The compatibility of the framing with the quantum Satake equivalence was proved by Bezrukavnikov–Finkelberg–Mirković for simply connected groups and extended to non‑simply connected groups by Braverman–Finkelberg–Nakajima using the theory of categorical characters.

The Geometric Langlands Correspondence and the Theory of the Geometric Langlands Correspondence for the Moduli of G‑Bundles with a Level Structure and the Loop Group in Mixed Characteristic

Let R be a complete discrete valuation ring with fraction field of characteristic zero and residue field of characteristic p > 0. A level structure of level N on a G-bundle over a curve X defined over R is a trivialisation of the bundle on the N-th infinitesimal neighbourhood of a point x. The moduli stack BunG,N(X) carries an action of the parahoric loop group G[[t]]N. In mixed characteristic the nearby cycles functor on the relative Hitchin fibration produces a complex on the special fibre whose cohomology carries an action of the inertia group of the local field. The geometric Langlands correspondence in this setting, formulated by Zhu and by Bouthier–Kazhdan–Varshavsky, identifies the category of D-modules on BunG,N with the category of ind‑coherent sheaves on the derived stack of Ĝ-local systems with a prescribed level‑N structure, equipped with a compatible action of the inertia group. The categorical trace of the level‑N Hecke functors computes the partition function of the four‑dimensional N = 4 super Yang–Mills theory with a surface defect labelled by the parahoric subgroup, and the Verlinde formula expresses this partition function in terms of the dimensions of spaces of conformal blocks for the quantum group Uq(Ĝ) at a root of unity determined by the level and the residue characteristic. The mixed‑characteristic Prym variety appears as the Hitchin fibre for G = GLn with fixed determinant, and its nearby cycles monodromy encodes the wild ramification of the corresponding Ĝ-local system.

  • Confusing the mixed‑characteristic Prym variety with its equal‑characteristic analogue; the nearby cycles functor introduces a monodromy operator absent in characteristic zero.
  • Assuming the critical level centre is unchanged in positive characteristic; the Frobenius twist modifies the Feigin–Frenkel isomorphism and forces the oper condition.
  • Neglecting the component group action on the spherical Hecke category for non‑simply connected groups; it is essential for the framed equivalence.
  • Treating wild ramification in mixed characteristic as a straightforward extension of the tame case; the (φ,Γ)-module formalism and Stokes filtrations are fundamentally different.
  • Overlooking the role of the unipotent radical in the parabolic automorphic category; equivariance under U is required for the correct Hecke eigenvalues.
  • Believing the Ngô support theorem holds verbatim for the wild Hitchin fibration; the singularities of wild fibers demand a refined nearby cycles analysis.
  • Assuming the quantum K‑theoretic R‑matrix coincides with the quantum cohomology R‑matrix without accounting for the equivariant parameter ℏ and the K‑theoretic Novikov variable.
